Running default agent with env variables set, all the functionality from the twitter interactions seems to work except for the replies. The action taken is actually IGNORE instead of REPLY 

Default config and settings Version: 1.0.4

Am i missing something?1a:T78cd,**Describe the bug**

X bot doesn't reply to any mentions at all. Polling works, Posting works, but bot ignores all mentions.

**To Reproduce**

- Install elizaOS
- add twitter logins and LLM API on .env (also using plugin-dkg but that does not trigger when my bot does not manage to reply to mentions)
- run a character

**Expected behavior**

Replying to TWITTER_TARGET_USERS when mentioned. However, I see INSTRUCTIONS on the logs but nothing happens and LLM does not make a decision, and then INSTRUCTIONS keep on looping. Logs also say already responded to tweet but bot did not answer anything. Polling seems to work, actions like retweeting and liking are fine, but responding to mentions do not work.

1c:Ta9b,**Describe the bug**

https://eliza.how/docs/quickstart -> Install Globally (Recommended)

There are two main issues affecting the onboarding and first interaction with ElizaOS:

1. **Missing prerequisites in documentation:**  
   The Quickstart guide does not mention that [Bun](https://bun.sh/) is required for plugin installation, nor does it mention that some essential plugins (such as `@elizaos/plugin-sql`) may need to be installed manually if the automatic installation fails. This leads to startup errors for new users.

2. **Error when interacting with the agent:**  
   After installing Bun and the required plugins, the dashboard loads, but when trying to interact with the agent, an error occurs:  
   `Error: No handler found for delegate type: TEXT_EMBEDDING`

---

**To Reproduce**

1. Follow the Quickstart guide:  
   - Install ElizaOS CLI with `npm install -g @elizaos/cli@beta`
   - Run `elizaos start`
3. Select your database: › pglite (embedded database)
3. Observe errors about missing plugins (e.g., `@elizaos/plugin-sql`) and failed automatic installation.
4. Manually install missing plugins and Bun as a workaround.
5. Start ElizaOS again, open the dashboard at `http://localhost:3000`.
6. Try to interact with the agent.
7. Observe the error:  
   `Error: No handler found for delegate type: TEXT_EMBEDDING`

---

**Expected behavior**

- The Quickstart guide should mention all prerequisites, including Bun, and provide troubleshooting steps for missing plugins.
- After installing all dependencies, the agent should be able to process messages without errors related to missing embedding handlers.

---

1e:Te70,When you send a message, two messages get added to the chat immediately: one from the user and a second from the "system" with a loading indicator. 
But if the API call fails (like due to no internet), both fake messages stay in the chat as if nothing went wrong. There’s no rollback, no error handling on the UI so it looks like the message was sent when it actually wasn’t.

To Reproduce:

1.Open the chat page (eliza/client/src/components/chat.tsx)

2.Disconnect from the internet or block the request to the send message endpoint.

3.Type a message and hit Enter.

4.You’ll see your message and a “loading” message instantly.

5.Even though the request fails, both messages stay on screen.

Expected behavior
If the message fails to send, the temporary messages should be removed automatically. The user should also get feedback (like a toast or error icon) to let them know it didn’t work.

Additional context
To fix it, I added a tempId to identify and clean up fake messages properly. Now if the message fails, those temporary entries get removed from the cache.


In chat.tsx, update the type:
```
type ExtraContentFields = {
    user: string;
    createdAt: number;
    isLoading?: boolean;
    tempId?: string; // ✅ for cleanup
};

```
Replace handleSendMessage with:
```
const handleSendMessage = (e: React.FormEvent<HTMLFormElement>) => {
    e.preventDefault();
    if (!input) return;

    const tempId = `${Date.now()}-${Math.random()}`;
    const attachments: IAttachment[] | undefined = selectedFile
        ? [
              {
                  url: URL.createObjectURL(selectedFile),
                  contentType: selectedFile.type,
                  title: selectedFile.name,
              },
          ]
        : undefined;

    const tempMessages: ContentWithUser[] = [
        {
            text: input,
            user: "user",
            createdAt: Date.now(),
            attachments,
            isLoading: true,
            tempId,
        },
        {
            text: input,
            user: "system",
            createdAt: Date.now(),
            isLoading: true,
            tempId,
        },
    ];

    queryClient.setQueryData(
        ["messages", agentId],
        (old: ContentWithUser[] = []) => [...old, ...tempMessages]
    );

    sendMessageMutation.mutate({
        message: input,
        selectedFile,
        tempId,
    });

    setSelectedFile(null);
    setInput("");
    formRef.current?.reset();
};
```

And update the mutation like this:
```
const sendMessageMutation = useMutation({
    mutationKey: ["send_message", agentId],
    mutationFn: ({
        message,
        selectedFile,
    }: {
        message: string;
        selectedFile?: File | null;
        tempId: string;
    }) => apiClient.sendMessage(agentId, message, selectedFile),
    onSuccess: (newMessages: ContentWithUser[], variables) => {
        const { tempId } = variables;
        queryClient.setQueryData(
            ["messages", agentId],
            (old: ContentWithUser[] = []) => [
                ...old.filter((msg) => msg.tempId !== tempId),
                ...newMessages.map((msg) => ({
                    ...msg,
                    createdAt: Date.now(),
                })),
            ]
        );
    },
    onError: (error, variables) => {
        const { tempId } = variables;
        queryClient.setQueryData(
            ["messages", agentId],
            (old: ContentWithUser[] = []) =>
                old.filter((msg) => msg.tempId !== tempId)
        );

        toast({
            variant: "destructive",
            title: "Unable to send message",
            description: error.message,
        });
    },
});
```

```22:T672,**Describe the bug**

The API endpoint `/api/agents/:agentId/rooms` returns an empty list of rooms `({"success":true,"data":{"rooms":[]}})` even though the agent is actively participating in multiple rooms and responding to messages in those rooms.

**To Reproduce**

Start the agent with multiple connected services _(Discord, Telegram, etc.)_
Verify the agent is responding to messages in rooms _(e.g., Discord channels)_
Call the API endpoint **GET** `/api/agents/:agentId/rooms`
Observe that the response contains an empty rooms array despite the agent being active in rooms

**Expected behavior**

The API should return a list of all rooms where the agent is a participant. For example:
```
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "rooms": [
      {
        "id": "room-id-1",
        "name": "Room Name 1",
        "source": "discord",
        ...
      },
      ...
    ]
  }
}
```

**Additional context**

- In the agent logs, there are numerous successful room creations: `Room [UUID] created successfully`
- Agent connection to rooms is also logged: Success: `Successfully connected entity [agentId] in room [roomId]`
- When a message is received and processed in a room, the agent correctly responds
- The logs show an error: `ERROR: No room found for settings provider` which might be related
- The issue appears to be in the association between the agent and rooms in the database, or in how `getRoomsForParticipant` retrieves this information
- The function that calls this API is in `eliza/packages/cli/src/server/api/agent.ts`, which delegates to `runtime.getRoomsForParticipant(agentId)` which in turn delegates to the database adapter

25:T1f21,**Describe the bug**

I'm creating a custom plugin to generate images using comfy.icu api. I've added action to generate a picture, but whenever I call a `callback` from action with proper response, the web chat receives completely different message, even if the action below the message shows proper action, but the content is completely different. And the `providers` array has `ATTACHMENTS` entry.

**To Reproduce**

Here's my code:
`import type { HandlerCallback, IAgentRuntime, Memory, Plugin, State } from "@elizaos/core";
import { logger } from "@elizaos/core";
import { fetch } from "undici";

/**
 * Gets a configuration setting from the runtime or environment
 */
function getSetting(
  runtime: IAgentRuntime,
  key: string,
  defaultValue?: string
): string | undefined {
  return runtime.getSetting(key) ?? process.env[key] ?? defaultValue;
}

/**
 * Gets the Comfy.icu API key
 */
function getApiKey(runtime: IAgentRuntime): string | undefined {
  return getSetting(runtime, "COMFY_ICU_API_KEY");
}

/**
 * Executes a workflow on comfy.icu and returns the result
 */
async function executeWorkflow(
  runtime: IAgentRuntime,
  workflowId: string,
  params: Record<string, any> = {}
): Promise<{ success: boolean; imageUrl?: string; error?: string }> {
  const apiKey = getApiKey(runtime);

  if (!apiKey) {
    logger.error("COMFY_ICU_API_KEY is not set");
    return { success: false, error: "API key not configured" };
  }

  try {
    // Trigger the workflow
    const response = await fetch(
      `https://comfy.icu/api/v1/workflows/${workflowId}/runs`,
      {
        method: "POST",
        headers: {
          "Content-Type": "application/json",
          Authorization: `Bearer ${apiKey}`,
        },
        body: JSON.stringify(params),
      }
    );

    if (!response.ok) {
      const errorText = await response.text();
      logger.error(
        `Failed to trigger workflow: ${response.status} - ${errorText}`
      );
      return {
        success: false,
        error: `Workflow execution failed: ${response.status} - ${errorText}`,
      };
    }

    const result = (await response.json()) as {
      id: string;
    };

    if (!result.id) {
      logger.error("Workflow execution failed: Unknown error");
      return { success: false, error: "Unknown error" };
    }

    // Poll for completion
    const runId = result.id;
    const imageUrl = await pollForCompletion(runtime, workflowId, runId);

    if (!imageUrl) {
      return { success: false, error: "Failed to retrieve generated image" };
    }

    return { success: true, imageUrl };
  } catch (error) {
    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
    logger.error(`Error executing workflow: ${message}`);
    return { success: false, error: `Error: ${message}` };
  }
}

/**
 * Polls the comfy.icu API for workflow completion
 */
async function pollForCompletion(
  runtime: IAgentRuntime,
  workflowId: string,
  runId: string
): Promise<string | null> {
  const apiKey = getApiKey(runtime);
  const maxAttempts = 30; // 5 minutes with 10-second intervals
  let attempts = 0;

  while (attempts < maxAttempts) {
    try {
      const response = await fetch(
        `https://comfy.icu/api/v1/workflows/${workflowId}/runs/${runId}`,
        {
          headers: {
            Authorization: `Bearer ${apiKey}`,
          },
        }
      );

      if (!response.ok) {
        logger.warn(`Status check failed: ${response.status}`);
        await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 10000)); // Wait 10 seconds
        attempts++;
        continue;
      }

      const status = (await response.json()) as {
        status: "STARTED" | "COMPLETED" | "ERROR";
        output?: Array<{ url: string }>;
        error?: string;
      };

      if (
        status.status === "COMPLETED" &&
        status.output &&
        status.output.length > 0
      ) {
        // Get the first image URL
        const imageUrl = status.output[0].url;
        if (imageUrl) {
          logger.info(`Workflow completed, image URL: ${imageUrl}`);
          return imageUrl;
        } else {
          logger.error("Workflow completed but no image URL found");
          return null;
        }
      } else if (status.status === "ERROR") {
        logger.error(`Workflow failed: ${status.error || "Unknown error"}`);
        return null;
      }

      // Still processing, wait and try again
      logger.debug(`Workflow status: ${status.status}, waiting...`);
      await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 10000)); // Wait 10 seconds
      attempts++;
    } catch (error) {
      const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
      logger.error(`Error checking workflow status: ${message}`);
      await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 10000)); // Wait 10 seconds
      attempts++;
    }
  }

  logger.error("Timed out waiting for workflow completion");
  return null;
}

/**
 * Comfy.icu plugin for ElizaOS
 */
export const comfyIcuPlugin: Plugin = {
  name: "comfy-icu",
  description: "Comfy.icu integration for ElizaOS",
  config: {
    COMFY_ICU_API_KEY: process.env.COMFY_ICU_API_KEY,
    WORKFLOW_ID_SFW: process.env.WORKFLOW_ID_SFW,
    WORKFLOW_ID_NSFW: process.env.WORKFLOW_ID_NSFW,
  },
  async init(_config, runtime) {
    if (!getApiKey(runtime)) {
      logger.warn(
        "COMFY_ICU_API_KEY is not set - Comfy.icu functionality will be limited"
      );
      return;
    }

    logger.info("Comfy.icu plugin initialized");
  },
  actions: [
    {
      name: "GENERATE_IMAGE",
      description: "Generates an image based on the provided description using comfy.icu API",
      similes: [
        "CREATE_IMAGE",
        "IMAGE_GENERATOR",
        "PICTURE_CREATOR",
        "COMFY_IMAGE",
      ],
      examples: [
        [
          {
            name: "{{user1}}",
            content: {
              text: "Generate an image of a sunset over mountains",
            },
          },
          {
            name: "{{agentName}}",
            content: {
              text: "Here's an image of a sunset over mountains:",
              actions: ["GENERATE_IMAGE"],
              attachements: [{id: "2242142-fasa-323-asd3d", url: "https://comfy.icu/generated-image.jpg"}],
            },
          },
        ],
        [
          {
            name: "{{user1}}",
            content: {
              text: "Can you create a picture of a futuristic city?",
            },
          },
          {
            name: "{{agentName}}",
            content: {
              text: "Here's an image of a futuristic city:",
              actions: ["GENERATE_IMAGE"],
              attachements: [{id: "2242142-fasa-323-asd3d", url: "https://comfy.icu/generated-image.jpg"}],
            },
          },
        ],
      ],
      validate: async (runtime: IAgentRuntime, message: Memory, state?: State): Promise<boolean> => {
        return true;
      },
      handler: async (runtime: IAgentRuntime, message: Memory, state?: State, options?: any, callback?: HandlerCallback): Promise<boolean> => {
        logger.info("Generating image using Comfy.icu...");
        logger.info('Message callback:' + typeof callback);

        const responseContent = {
          text: 'Here is your generated image:',
          actions: ['GENERATE_IMAGE'],
          attachments: [
            {
              id: '1234f-fasd-32fsa-1234f',
              url: 'https://some-domain.com/custom-picture.png',
            }
          ]
        };

        if (callback) {
          await callback(responseContent);
        }

        return true;
    },
  ],
};

export default comfyIcuPlugin;

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->2c:T560,### Integrate Ethereum Name Service to WalletLinkForm.
On /profile/edit page, Instead of inputting user's full wallet address, user will also be able to reference one's wallet address by providing an ENS domain. 
Considering the risk that an ENS domain gets expired or transferred and the user neglects to adjust one's README.md hidden comment, ENS is only used for initially getting the corresponding wallet address on WalletLinkForm, anything beyond resolves to the raw address.
![ens](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/edb39bda-5e71-4316-bb83-7977e6a87ab4)

If this flow makes sense, the same can perhaps be done for SNS (Solana Name Service)

Key updates include the introduction of a new utility for detecting local plugin development contexts, enhancements to the message bus service for better handling of direct messages, and the removal of redundant scripts in the client package.

### Plugin Development Enhancements:

33:T9b9,# Risks

**Medium** - Changes to SocketIO event handling and API routing
- Could affect message synchronization across clients
- Routing changes might impact plugin functionality
- Client-side state management modifications

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Add messageDeleted and channelCleared SocketIO events
- Implement client-side handlers for real-time UI updates  
- Fix routing to exclude /api/messages/ from plugin handler
- Add DELETE /central-channels/:channelId endpoint
- Clean up debug logs and improve TypeScript types
- Prevent 404 errors when deleting channels

Fixes message deletion requiring page refresh

## What kind of change is this?

**Bug fixes** (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)
**Improvements** (misc. changes to existing features)

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

Users experienced poor UX where deleted messages remained visible in the chat interface until manually refreshing the page. The DELETE API worked correctly but the UI wasn't receiving real-time updates via SocketIO events.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Review SocketIO event handling in `socketio-manager.ts` and `use-socket-chat.ts`
2. Check routing exclusion logic in `eliza/packages/cli/src/server/api/index.ts`
3. Verify new DELETE endpoint in `messages.ts`

## Detailed testing steps

- Start an agent and open the chat interface
- Send several messages in a DM or group chat
- Delete a message using the delete button
  - Verify message disappears immediately without page refresh
  - Verify SocketIO `messageDeleted` event is received
- Clear entire channel using clear button
  - Verify all messages disappear immediately
  - Verify SocketIO `channelCleared` event is received
- Delete a DM channel entirely
  - Verify no 404 errors occur
  - Verify proper API response

## Screenshots

### Before
- Messages remained visible after deletion until page refresh
- 404 errors when deleting channels
- Verbose debug logs with emojis

### After  
- Messages disappear instantly upon deletion
- Clean channel deletion without errors
- Cleaner log output
- Real-time UI updates via SocketIO events

# Deploy Notes

## Database changes
None - only UI and API routing changes

## Deployment instructions
Standard build and restart required:
```bash
cd eliza
bun run build:client && bun run build:cli
```

### Development Workflow Enhancements:

* Updated the `dev` script in `package.json` to use the new `dev-watch.js` script instead of the previous Turbo-based command.

* Adjusted the `logLevel` in `vite.config.ts` to show `info` logs during development for better debugging visibility.
* Added a script to `index.html` that dynamically loads a development refresh script when running on localhost, improving the developer experience.

### Build and Start Script Updates:
* Simplified the `start` and `build` scripts in `package.json` by removing unnecessary flags, aligning them with the updated development workflow.
* Updated `turbo.json` to remove the `cache: false` configuration for `start` and `dev` tasks, simplifying task definitions.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->3b:T62d,# PR Description: Switch to postgres-js for improved Supabase compatibility

## Summary
Migrated database connection from node-postgres to postgres-js to resolve compatibility issues users were experiencing with Supabase deployments.

## Changes Made
- Switched from `drizzle-orm/node-postgres` to `drizzle-orm/postgres-js`
- Updated import statements to use `postgres` from 'postgres' package instead of node-postgres Pool
- Added `prepare: false` configuration to disable prepared statements for Supabase transaction pooling mode
- Updated dependency requirements in package.json

## Why This Change
- Supabase's transaction pooling mode doesn't support prepared statements, which was causing connection issues for users
- postgres-js uses prepared statements by default, but allows easy opt-out for environments like Supabase
- postgres-js is the recommended driver for Supabase integration with Drizzle ORM

## Benefits
- Eliminates connection errors when using Supabase's connection pooler
- Better compatibility with Supabase's transaction mode pooling
- Maintains all existing functionality while improving reliability
- Follows Supabase + Drizzle best practices from official documentation

## Testing
- [ ] Verified connection works with Supabase transaction pooling
- [ ] Confirmed existing queries continue to function
- [ ] Tested with both direct connections and pooled connections

This change should resolve the Supabase connection issues users have been experiencing while maintaining backward compatibility with existing implementations.3c:T70c,## 📋 Summary

## 🚨 Problem

When plugin developers run `elizaos test` from within their plugin directory, the CLI encounters a critical circular dependency issue:

1. **Test Command Execution**: `elizaos test` is run from within a plugin directory (e.g., `/path/to/my-plugin/`)
2. **Plugin Loading Attempt**: The CLI attempts to load the plugin being tested
3. **Loading Failure**: Plugin not found in node_modules (since it's being developed locally)
4. **Installation Trigger**: System calls `installPlugin()` to install the missing plugin
5. **Self-Installation**: Plugin tries to install itself into its own directory
6. **Circular Dependency**: 💥 Creates circular reference, corrupts package.json, and breaks testing

### Real-World Impact
- Plugin developers cannot run tests without encountering errors
- Package.json files get corrupted by self-installation attempts
- Development workflow is severely hindered
- Poor developer experience discourages plugin development

## 🔍 Root Cause Analysis

The core issue lies in the plugin loading system's lack of **context awareness**:

### Current Flow (Problematic)
```
elizaos test (in plugin dir) 
  → startAgent() 
  → loadAndPreparePlugin("@user/plugin-name")
  → loadPluginModule() [FAILS - not in node_modules]
  → installPlugin("@user/plugin-name", process.cwd()) [CIRCULAR\!]
  → Package corruption & dependency errors
```

### Key Issues Identified
1. **No Local Development Detection**: System doesn't recognize when operating within a plugin's own directory
2. **Blind Installation Logic**: `installPlugin()` doesn't prevent self-installation
3. **Missing Context**: No distinction between "external plugin" vs "current plugin being tested"
4. **Poor Error Handling**: Generic error messages provide no actionable guidance

## ✅ Solution: Context-Aware Plugin Resolution

This PR introduces a comprehensive **context-aware plugin resolution system** that intelligently handles local plugin development scenarios.

### New Architecture

```
elizaos test (in plugin dir)
  → detectPluginContext() [NEW: Smart detection]
  → IF local development detected:
    → Load from local dist/ [PREVENTS circular dependency]
    → Auto-build if needed
    → Provide helpful guidance
  → ELSE:
    → Continue with normal plugin installation flow
```

## 🛠 Implementation Details

### 1. Plugin Context Detection System

**New File**: `src/utils/plugin-context.ts`

```typescript
export function detectPluginContext(pluginName: string): PluginContext {
  // Intelligent detection of local plugin development
  // Handles various naming conventions and directory structures
  // Returns context with build status and local paths
}
```

**Features**:
- **Smart Name Matching**: Handles `@scope/plugin-name`, `plugin-name`, `name` variations
- **Directory Fallback**: Matches by directory name when package names differ
- **Build Detection**: Checks if plugin is built and ready to load
- **Path Resolution**: Provides exact paths for local loading

### 2. Enhanced Plugin Loading Strategy

**Modified**: `src/utils/load-plugin.ts`

**New Priority Order**:
1. **🎯 Local Development Plugin** (NEW - Highest Priority)
2. Workspace dependency
3. Direct path
4. Local node_modules
5. Global node_modules
6. Package.json entry
7. Common dist pattern

**Local Development Strategy**:
- Detects plugin development context
- Auto-builds plugin if needed
- Loads directly from `dist/` output
- Provides helpful error messages
- Skips unnecessary installation attempts

### 3. Self-Installation Prevention

**Modified**: `src/utils/install-plugin.ts` & `src/commands/start.ts`

```typescript
// NEW: Early detection prevents circular dependency
const context = detectPluginContext(pluginName);
if (context.isLocalDevelopment) {
  // Block self-installation
  // Provide helpful guidance
  // Load locally instead
}
```

**Benefits**:
- **Prevents Corruption**: No more package.json modification attempts
- **Clear Messaging**: Explains why installation was prevented
- **Alternative Path**: Suggests local build and provides commands

### 4. Improved Developer Experience

**Enhanced Error Handling**:
- Context-aware error messages
- Specific build instructions
- Actionable guidance for developers
- Clear explanation of detection logic

Fixed agent cross-chat interference in DM channels where multiple agents would respond to messages intended for a single agent. The root cause was incorrect metadata preservation causing `agent_response` messages to lose their DM context, leading to unwanted cross-agent chatter.

## Root Cause Analysis

1. **Context Bleeding**: Agent responses were being sent with `isDm: false` even when responding to DM messages
2. **Inconsistent Channel Type Metadata**: Original message metadata wasn't being preserved through response chains
3. **Missing DM Filtering**: `agent_response` messages weren't being filtered based on channel type in the message bus service

## Technical Solution

### 1. Enhanced Metadata Preservation (`message.ts:390-392`)

Fixed the `sendAgentResponseToBus` method to preserve original message metadata:

```typescript
metadata: {
  agent_id: this.runtime.agentId,
  agentName: this.runtime.character.name,
  attachments: content.attachments,
  channelType: originalMessage?.metadata?.channelType || room?.type,
  isDm: originalMessage?.metadata?.isDm || (originalMessage?.metadata?.channelType || room?.type) === ChannelType.DM,
},
```

### 2. Agent Response Filtering (`message.ts:144-150`)

Added targeted filtering to prevent cross-agent interference:

```typescript
// Skip agent_response messages from other agents in DM channels to prevent cross-agent chatter
if (message.source_type === 'agent_response' && (message.metadata?.channelType === ChannelType.DM || message.metadata?.isDm)) {
  logger.debug(
    `[${this.runtime.character.name}] MessageBusService: Skipping agent_response message in DM channel to prevent cross-agent interference.`
  );
  return;
}
```

### 3. ChannelType Enum Standardization

Standardized ChannelType values across the codebase:
- **Main types file**: Updated to use consistent uppercase values (`DM`, `GROUP` instead of `dm`, `group`)
- **V2 types file**: Synchronized enum values for consistency
- **Test files**: Updated to use enum constants instead of hardcoded strings

## Impact

✅ **Prevents**: Unwanted agent-to-agent responses in DM channels  
✅ **Preserves**: Group chat functionality where multiple agents should interact  
✅ **Maintains**: Proper context throughout conversation threads  
✅ **Ensures**: Consistent channel type handling across the codebase  

## Files Modified

- `/packages/cli/src/server/services/message.ts` - Core message routing fixes
- `/packages/core/src/types.ts` - ChannelType enum standardization  
- `/packages/core/src/specs/v2/types.ts` - Synchronized enum values
- `/packages/plugin-bootstrap/__tests__/evaluators.test.ts` - Updated test to use enum

## Testing

The fix has been tested and verified to:
- Stop cross-agent responses in DM channels
- Maintain proper agent behavior in group channels
- Preserve conversation context through message threads

  - `-h, --help`: Displays help information. (`packages/cli/src/index.ts`, [packages/cli/src/index.tsL71-R78](diffhunk://#diff-2fb448d11702324cc59d657d5945d59cd325540eb87b71f2e66a5bc01ada834cL71-R78))

* Updated the `README.md` to document the automatic Bun installation feature, including how to disable it using flags or environment variables. (`packages/cli/README.md`, [packages/cli/README.mdR11-R31](diffhunk://#diff-15986190ef9581ab59bcd5483b2c09e7fd0bd439d6f6cddbc94b0b1de094ee51R11-R31))

## Problem

**Missing TypeScript Declaration Files in Published Plugins**

- Plugins created from the `plugin-starter` template were missing `.d.ts` files when published to npm
- This caused poor TypeScript developer experience with no autocompletion, type checking, or IntelliSense
- Published packages only contained `index.js` and `index.js.map` but no `index.d.ts`

**Inconsistent Documentation and Tooling**

- README files referenced outdated npm-based commands instead of current bun/elizaos CLI workflow
- .gitignore files were inconsistent across starter templates
- npm publishing configuration wasn't including all necessary files

## Solution

### 1. Fixed TypeScript Declaration Generation

**Root Cause**: Conflicting TypeScript configurations prevented `.d.ts` file generation:

```diff
// tsup.config.ts - Was preventing declaration generation
- dts: false, // Skip DTS generation to avoid external import issues
+ dts: true, // require DTS so we get d.ts in the dist folder on npm
```

```diff
// tsconfig.json & tsconfig.build.json - Had conflicting settings
- "emitDeclarationOnly": true, // Conflicted with tsup bundling
- "rootDir": "./src", // Too restrictive for monorepo imports
- "allowImportingTsExtensions": true, // Incompatible without emitDeclarationOnly
+ "allowImportingTsExtensions": false, // Fixed compatibility
```

### 2. Fixed npm Publishing Configuration

**Ensured proper file inclusion**:

```diff
// package.json - Added missing files for npm publishing
"files": [
  "dist",
+ "README.md",
+ ".npmignore",
+ "package.json",
+ "tsup.config.ts"
]
```

```diff
// .npmignore - Removed dist exclusion so compiled code gets published
- dist
```

### 3. Standardized Development Workflow Documentation

**Updated commands to reflect current tooling**:

```bash
# Before (outdated)
npx elizaos create my-project
npm install
npm run dev
npm run test

# After (current)
elizaos create -t plugin my-awesome-feature  # Auto-adds plugin- prefix
cd plugin-my-awesome-feature               # Dependencies auto-installed
elizaos dev                                # Hot-reloading ready
elizaos test                               # Runs all tests
```

### 4. Standardized .gitignore Files

Applied comprehensive and consistent `.gitignore` patterns across all starter templates:

- Environment files (`.env*`)
- OS files (`.DS_Store`, `Thumbs.db`)
- IDE files (`.vscode/`, `.idea/`)
- Build artifacts and cache directories
- ElizaOS-specific directories (`.elizadb/`, `pglite/`, `cache/`)

## Impact

**✅ Fixed TypeScript Support**

- New plugins now automatically include `index.d.ts` in published packages
- TypeScript users get full autocompletion and type checking
- Plugins work seamlessly in both JS and TS projects

**✅ Improved Developer Experience**

- Documentation reflects actual current workflow
- Consistent ignore patterns across all templates
- Clear guidance on when to use `elizaos dev` vs `elizaos start`

**✅ Proper npm Package Structure**
Published plugins now include all necessary files:

```
dist/
├── index.js       ✅ Bundled code
├── index.js.map   ✅ Source maps
└── index.d.ts     ✅ TypeScript declarations (FIXED!)
```

This resolves the core issue where plugin developers were publishing incomplete packages without TypeScript support, while also modernizing the development workflow documentation to match current ElizaOS tooling.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->45:T13a7,# Fix CLI update from npm <1.0.5 to bun 1.0.6 by auto-migrating installations

## Problem

Users with npm-installed CLI version <1.0.5 cannot update to version 1.0.6+ because ElizaOS switched from npm to bun as the primary package manager. When attempting to update, the CLI fails to find newer versions because:

1. The npm-installed CLI tries to update via npm
2. ElizaOS 1.0.6+ is only published to bun's registry/installation method
3. Users get stuck on <1.0.5 with no clear upgrade path

### Reproduction

```bash
# User has npm-installed CLI 1.0.5
$ elizaos -v
1.0.5

# Attempt to update via the CLI
$ elizaos update --cli
Checking for CLI updates...
CLI is already at the latest version (1.0.5) [✓]

# Manual check shows newer version exists
$ npm view @elizaos/cli version
1.0.6

# But CLI can't access it due to package manager mismatch
```

### Environment Detection

```bash
# Users may have both npm and bun versions installed
$ which -a elizaos
/Users/user/.bun/bin/elizaos     # bun version (1.0.6)
/usr/local/bin/elizaos           # npm version (1.0.5)

# PATH priority determines which runs by default
$ elizaos -v
1.0.5  # Running npm version despite bun version being available
```

## Solution

Implement automatic migration from npm to bun installation during CLI updates:

1. **Detection**: Check if CLI is installed via npm using `npm list -g @elizaos/cli`
2. **Atomic Migration**: Install bun version first, then remove npm version
3. **Verification**: Ensure bun installation works before removing npm
4. **Fallback**: If migration fails, fallback to standard npm update
5. **Safety**: Preserve npm installation on any failure to avoid leaving user without CLI

### Implementation

#### Migration Utilities (`cli-bun-migration.ts`)

- `isCliInstalledViaNpm()` - Detects npm installations including nvm scenarios
- `installCliWithBun()` - Installs CLI via bun with proper error handling
- `removeNpmInstallation()` - Safely removes npm installation
- `migrateCliToBun()` - Orchestrates atomic migration process

#### Update Command Integration

- Check for npm installation before standard update flow
- Attempt migration if npm installation detected
- Fallback to standard update if migration fails
- Preserve all existing update functionality

### Migration Flow

```bash
# User runs update command
$ elizaos update --cli

# System detects npm installation
Detected npm installation, migrating to bun...

# Atomic migration process
✓ Checking bun availability
✓ Installing CLI via bun (1.0.6)
✓ Verifying bun installation works
✓ Removing npm installation
✓ CLI updated successfully to version 1.0.6

# User now has bun-managed CLI
$ elizaos -v
1.0.6
```

### Error Handling

```bash
# If migration fails at any point
$ elizaos update --cli
Detected npm installation, migrating to bun...
✗ Migration to bun failed, falling back to standard npm update...
Updating CLI from 1.0.5 to 1.0.6...
✓ CLI updated successfully to version 1.0.6
```

## Changes

### New Files

- `packages/cli/src/utils/cli-bun-migration.ts` - Migration utility functions

### Modified Files

- `packages/cli/src/commands/update.ts` - Integrated migration logic into update flow

## Testing

### Manual Testing Scenarios

1. **npm-installed CLI**: Verify migration works correctly
2. **bun-installed CLI**: Verify standard update flow unchanged
3. **No CLI installation**: Verify new installations work
4. **Migration failure**: Verify fallback to npm update works
5. **Multiple installations**: Verify correct detection and handling

### Commands to Test

```bash
# Test detection
npm list -g @elizaos/cli

# Test migration
elizaos update --cli

# Verify result
elizaos -v
which elizaos
```

## Benefits

- **Seamless Migration**: Users automatically get moved to bun without manual intervention
- **No Broken State**: Atomic migration ensures users always have working CLI
- **Backward Compatibility**: Existing workflows continue to work
- **Future-Proof**: New users automatically get bun installation
- **Safe Fallbacks**: Multiple fallback mechanisms prevent user abandonment

## Risk Mitigation

- **Atomic Operations**: Install bun first, only remove npm on success
- **Verification Steps**: Confirm bun installation works before cleanup
- **Fallback Logic**: Standard npm update if migration fails
- **Error Handling**: Comprehensive error handling with user-friendly messages
- **Non-Destructive**: Never leaves user without a working CLI installation

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->46:Tb8f,## Description

This PR includes two major enhancements to the ElizaOS plugin system:

1. **Enhanced plugin loading with direct import strategy for @elizaos packages**
2. **Automatic caret prefix for npm package versions during installation**

## Changes

### 1. Enhanced Plugin Loading (`packages/cli/src/utils/load-plugin.ts`)

Significantly improved the plugin loading system with better support for monorepos and npm packages:

- **Added direct import as the first strategy for `@elizaos/` scoped packages** - This ensures proper loading in monorepo environments with strict exports
- **Implemented workspace-aware node_modules resolution** - Automatically finds the workspace root and resolves modules from there
- **Added npm resolution using Node's native `require.resolve`** - Leverages Node's built-in module resolution algorithm
- **Prioritized import strategies** for better reliability:
  1. Direct import for `@elizaos` packages (best for monorepos)
  2. NPM resolved path using Node's resolution
  3. NPM package.json entry point
  4. Local package.json entry
  5. NPM common dist pattern
  6. Local common dist pattern
  7. Local node_modules root
  8. Workspace node_modules root

### 2. Version Handling Enhancement (`packages/cli/src/utils/package-manager.ts`)

Modified the `executeInstallation` function to automatically add caret (`^`) prefixes to npm package versions:

- Detects plain semantic versions (e.g., `1.0.0`)
- Automatically prepends caret (`^`) to create flexible version ranges (e.g., `^1.0.0`)
- Preserves existing range specifiers (`^`, `~`, `>`, `<`, `=`) without modification
- Excludes GitHub packages from this behavior (they use different syntax)

### 3. Additional Changes

- **Updated tests** to reflect the new version handling behavior
- **Cleaned up `bun.lock`** removing redundant entries

## Problem Solved

### Plugin Loading Issues
- `@elizaos/` scoped packages in monorepos often failed to load due to strict exports
- Module resolution didn't properly handle workspace hierarchies
- No fallback strategies when primary import methods failed

### Version Management Issues
- Exact versions from the plugin registry prevented automatic updates
- Missing bug fixes and compatible feature updates
- Required manual intervention for version updates

## Testing

- All existing tests pass
- Added comprehensive test cases for version handling
- Verified plugin loading in both monorepo and standalone environments

## Example Behavior

### Plugin Loading
```javascript
// Before: @elizaos/plugin-openai might fail in monorepo
// After: Direct import strategy ensures it loads correctly
```

### Version Installation
```javascript
// Before
npm install lodash@4.17.21  // Results in exact version

// After  
npm install lodash@^4.17.21  // Results in flexible version range
```

This PR ensures better reliability for plugin loading in various environments and improves dependency management for ElizaOS plugins.47:T8fc,<!-- Use this template by filling in information and copying and pasting relevant items out of the HTML comments. -->

48:T1771,# Simplify Monorepo Command and Update Docs & Tests

```

## PR Title

```
feat: simplify monorepo command and update documentation/tests
```

## Problem

The CLI's basic onboarding command had several UX and consistency issues that made it less intuitive for new users:

### 1. **Verbose Command Name**

- Command was named `setup-monorepo` which is unnecessarily long
- Most CLI tools use shorter, more memorable commands
- Inconsistent with the simplicity of other commands like `create`, `start`, `dev`

### 2. **Inconsistent Navigation Instructions**

- After cloning, the command showed absolute paths in navigation instructions
- Other commands like `create.ts` use relative paths (e.g., `cd myproject` instead of `cd /full/path/myproject`)
- This created inconsistent UX across the CLI

### 3. **Documentation Inconsistencies**

- Some docs referenced outdated default branches
- Installation instructions were inconsistent across different files
- Command references needed updating throughout documentation

## Solution

### 1. **Simplified Command Name**

- Renamed `setup-monorepo` → `monorepo`
- Updated all imports, exports, and registrations
- More intuitive: `elizaos monorepo` vs `elizaos setup-monorepo`

### 2. **Standardized Navigation Instructions**

- Updated `displayNextSteps()` function to use `path.relative()` like `create.ts`
- Now shows: `cd eliza` instead of `cd /Users/username/Desktop/eliza`
- Consistent UX across all CLI commands

### 3. **Comprehensive Documentation Updates**

- Updated all command references from `setup-monorepo` to `monorepo`
- Ensured all docs point to correct `develop` default branch
- Standardized installation instructions to use `bun i && bun run build`
- Updated CLI overview, README files, and command-specific documentation

- Implement smart strategy selection that checks file existence before attempting imports to find optimal path in one shot

- Make fallback strategy failures silent to reduce debug log noise while maintaining robust fallback system

## Problem
During elizaOS startup, the plugin loading system generates excessive debug logs and errors as it tries multiple import strategies sequentially before finding the correct one. This creates log spam and slower startup times with messages like:

```
[2025-06-01 13:42:55] DEBUG: Import failed using direct path ('@elizaos/plugin-bootstrap'):
[2025-06-01 13:42:55] DEBUG: Import failed using local node_modules ('/Users/studio/Documents/agi2/node_modules/@elizaos/plugin-bootstrap'):

```

## Solution
1. **Smart Strategy Selection**: Added `getOptimalStrategy()` function that checks file existence before attempting imports
2. **Optimized Strategy Order**: Reordered strategies to prioritize most likely successful paths:

   - common dist pattern (dist/index.js)
   - local node_modules directory
   - direct path
   - global node_modules (least likely)
3. **Silent Fallback Failures**: Made fallback strategy failures silent to reduce log noise

- [ ] Test plugin auto-installation workflow still works
- [ ] Verify fallback strategies still work for edge cases

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->51:T2097,# CLI Port Validation & Character File Handling Fix

## Problem

Two CLI issues were identified and fixed in this PR:

1. **Port validation** was happening at runtime instead of immediately during CLI argument parsing
2. **Character file handling** had edge cases that caused hanging and poor error messaging

---

## Issue 1: Port Validation

Port validation was happening at runtime instead of immediately during CLI argument parsing, causing poor user experience and preventing automation/CI/CD integration.

### Issues Identified

1. **Deferred Validation**: Commands like `dev` and `test` only validated port values when attempting to start servers, not during argument parsing
2. **Inconsistent Behavior**: Different commands had different validation approaches
3. **Poor User Experience**: Users had to wait for the entire startup process before receiving port validation errors
4. **Automation Impact**: Invalid ports caused delayed failures in CI/CD pipelines

### Example of the Problem

```bash
# Before the fix - validation happens at runtime
$ elizaos dev -p invalid_port_name
✅ Starting development server...
Building project...
Attempting to start server on port: invalid_port_name
Error binding to port: invalid_port_name

# Expected behavior - immediate validation
$ elizaos dev -p invalid_port_name
error: option '-p, --port <port>' argument 'invalid_port_name' is invalid. Port must be a number between 1 and 65535
```

### Solution

Implemented **immediate port validation** during CLI argument parsing by:

1. **Creating a reusable validation utility** (`port-validation.ts`)
2. **Standardizing validation across all commands** that accept port arguments
3. **Ensuring consistent error messaging** and user experience

---

## Issue 2: Character File Handling

### Issues Identified

1. **Hanging Behavior**: `elizaos start -char ""` hangs indefinitely instead of showing error message
2. **Missing Validation**: Empty character file parameters weren't validated before processing
3. **Poor Error Handling**: No graceful handling when character files are invalid

### Example of the Problem

```bash
# Before the fix - command hangs
$ elizaos start -char ""
# Hangs indefinitely with no feedback

# After the fix - clear error message
$ elizaos start -char ""
[WARN] Skipping empty character path
[ERROR] All provided character paths are empty or invalid. Starting with default character...
# Continues with default Eliza character
```

### Solution

Implemented **character file validation** before processing by:

1. **Early Validation**: Check for empty/invalid character paths before attempting to load them
2. **Clear Error Messages**: Provide specific warnings for empty paths and errors for invalid sets
3. **Graceful Fallback**: Continue with default character when all paths are invalid instead of hanging

---

## Implementation Details

### Files Modified

1. **`packages/cli/src/utils/port-validation.ts`** (new)

   - Extracted validation logic into reusable utility
   - Validates port range (1-65535)
   - Provides consistent error messaging

2. **`packages/cli/src/commands/start.ts`**

   - **Port**: Refactored to use the new validation utility
   - **Character**: Added validation for empty/invalid character file paths before processing
   - **Character**: Added graceful error handling and fallback to default character

3. **`packages/cli/src/commands/dev.ts`**

   - Added immediate port validation (was missing)
   - Updated help text to show default port

4. **`packages/cli/src/commands/test.ts`**
   - Added immediate port validation (was missing)
   - Updated help text to show default port

### Key Changes

#### Port Validation Utility (`port-validation.ts`)

```typescript
export function validatePort(value: string): number {
  const port = Number.parseInt(value, 10);
  if (Number.isNaN(port) || port <= 0 || port > 65535) {
    throw new Error('Port must be a number between 1 and 65535');
  }
  return port;
}
```

#### Character File Validation (`start.ts`)

```typescript
// Validate each part before adding to characterPaths
for (const part of parts) {
  if (!part.trim()) {
    logger.warn(`Skipping empty character path`);
  } else {
    characterPaths.push(part);
  }
}

// If no valid paths remain after filtering, show error
if (characterPaths.length === 0) {
  logger.error(
    `All provided character paths are empty or invalid. Starting with default character...`
  );
}
```

#### Command Updates

```typescript
// Before
.addOption(
  new Option('-p, --port <port>', 'Port to listen on').argParser((val) => Number.parseInt(val))
)

// After
.addOption(
  new Option('-p, --port <port>', 'Port to listen on (default: 3000)').argParser(validatePort)
)
```

52:T6c4,## Fix: Foreign Key Constraint Violation in ensureConnections

### Problem
The `ensureConnections` function was attempting to insert participants before ensuring the room exists, causing a foreign key constraint violation:

```
[2025-06-04 22:42:47] ERROR: Error adding participants
    entityIdSample: "b850bc30-45f8-0041-a00a-83df46d8555d"
    roomId: "77fea156-c7a3-05c2-8bd7-309f192a7514"
    agentId: "b850bc30-45f8-0041-a00a-83df46d8555d"
    error: "insert or update on table \"participants\" violates foreign key constraint \"participants_roomId_rooms_id_fk\""
```

### Root Cause
The foreign key constraint `participants_roomId_rooms_id_fk` enforces referential integrity between the `participants` and `rooms` tables. This constraint requires that any `roomId` in the `participants` table must reference an existing `id` in the `rooms` table.

The error occurred because the code was trying to insert participant records with a `roomId` that didn't exist yet in the `rooms` table.

### When This Issue Occurs
This is most obvious when having an agent participate in multiple Discord channels on the same server, where Discord channels map to rooms in the database. The race condition happens when the agent tries to join multiple channels simultaneously before the room records are properly created.

### Solution
Reordered the operations in `ensureConnections` to respect the foreign key constraint:

1. **First**: Create/ensure the room exists in the `rooms` table
2. **Then**: Create participants that reference that room

This ensures that parent records (rooms) are created before child records (participants) that reference them, maintaining referential integrity throughout the operation.53:T9ff,## 🔄 Add Retry Button for User Messages in Chat

## Breaking Changes
None - this is purely a cleanup that improves stability.58:T550,Refactors the core package's build process for improved modularity and maintainability.

- Adds dedicated entry points for different API versions.
- Updates the build configuration to use `tsup` for all build tasks.
- Enables declaration file generation for improved type safety.
- Excludes test files from the build output.

For npm modules, a single bundled index.js file is generally better for most use cases. Here's why:

## Benefits of Single Bundle
1. Performance
Fewer HTTP requests when loading the module
Faster module resolution (Node.js only needs to load one file)
Better caching (single file to cache)
Reduced I/O operations

2. Bundle Size Optimization
Dead code elimination during bundling
Better minification across the entire module
Shared code is deduplicated automatically
Tree-shaking works more effectively

3. Simpler Distribution
Single entry point makes imports cleaner
Easier to version and publish
Fewer files to manage in CI/CD
Cleaner npm package structure

4. Industry Standard
Most popular packages use this approach:

React: Single bundle
Lodash: Single bundle (with optional modular imports)
Axios: Single bundle
Express: Single bundle

5a:Tbfb,## Relates to

Resolves issue where MCP tool responses and other non-REPLY actions were generated but never sent to users.

## Risks

**Low** - This is a bug fix that ensures action callbacks are properly transmitted. The change is isolated to the Bootstrap plugin's `processActions` callback handling and only affects response transmission, not response generation.

## Background

### What does this PR do?

- Fix processActions callback not being transmitted to users for non-REPLY actions
- Actions like CALL_MCP_TOOL or any other actions now properly send responses to users via callback
- Only IGNORE/NONE actions now prevent responses, all others trigger shouldRespond=true
- Add debug logging for action callback execution and response message processing

### What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

## Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

## Testing

### Where should a reviewer start?

Review the changes in `eliza/packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/index.ts`:

1. **Line ~533-541**: The `processActions` callback now properly calls `await callback(memory)` to transmit responses to users
2. **Line ~107-112**: Updated `shouldRespond` logic to use blacklist approach (only IGNORE/NONE prevent responses) instead of hardcoded whitelist
3. **Added debug logging** throughout the action processing flow for better troubleshooting

### Detailed testing steps

**Before this fix:**
- Agent would announce starting the action (like calling MCP TOOL)
- In my exemple MCP tools would execute successfully and generate results
- Results would be logged in console / memory went created but never sent to the user
- User would wait indefinitely for a response

**After this fix:**
- Agent announces triggering the action
- MCP tools execute and generate results  
- Results are properly transmitted to the user via the callback
- User receives the search results as expected

**Test scenario:**
1. Set up agent with MCP plugin (e.g., search capabilities, Tavily for example)
2. Send message requesting a search: "Find me recent news about AI"
3. Verify agent responds with: announcement → actual search results
4. Check logs show both "Action callback called" and response transmission

**Manual verification:**
- Deploy agent with Bootstrap + MCP plugins
- Test various action types (CALL_MCP_TOOL, CONTINUE, etc.)
- Confirm all non-IGNORE/NONE actions properly send responses to users
- Verify debug logs show callback execution flow

If not an eliza-plugins official repo, i.e. new plugin: 

The plugin repo has:
- [ ] is publically accessible (not a private repo)
- [ ] uses main as it's default branch
- [ ] I have include `elizaos-plugins` in the topics in the GitHub repo settings. If the plugin is related to `AI` or `crypto`, please add those as topics as well.
- [ ] add simple description in github repo
- [ ] follows this convention
```
plugin-name/
├── images/
│   ├── logo.jpg        # Plugin branding logo
│   ├── banner.jpg      # Plugin banner image
├── src/
│   ├── index.ts        # Main plugin entry point
│   ├── actions/        # Plugin-specific actions
│   ├── clients/        # Client implementations
│   ├── adapters/       # Adapter implementations
│   └── types.ts        # Type definitions
│   └── environment.ts  # runtime.getSetting, zod validation
├── package.json        # Plugin dependencies
└── README.md          # Plugin documentation
```
- [ ] an `images/banner.jpg` and `images/logo.jpg` and they
  - Use clear, high-resolution images
  - Keep file sizes optimized (< 500KB for logos, < 1MB for banners)
  - Follow the [elizaOS Brand Guidelines](https://github.com/elizaOS/brandkit)
  - Include alt text for accessibility
- [ ] package.json has a agentConfig like the following
```json
{
  "name": "@myNpmOrg/plugin-example",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "agentConfig": {
    "pluginType": "elizaos:plugin:1.0.0",
    "pluginParameters": {
      "API_KEY": {
        "type": "string",
        "description": "API key for the service"
      }
    }
  }
}
```

61:T734,## Problem
- Empty strings in `elizaos plugins add ""` would trigger fuzzy search matching, installing random plugins (e.g., "plugin-0g")
- Help text for `plugins list` didn't clearly indicate that v1.x plugins are shown by default

## Solution
- **Empty String Validation**: Added `if (!pluginArg || !pluginArg.trim())` check before fuzzy search runs
- **Help Text Clarity**: Updated `plugins list` description to explicitly mention "(shows v1.x plugins by default)"
- **Documentation Updates**: Updated CLI README and plugin docs to reflect the v1.x default behavior

## Details
- **Root Cause**: Fuzzy search `cand.toLowerCase().includes(lower)` matches everything when `lower` is empty string
- **Validation Location**: Added at start of `plugins add` action before any processing
- **Error Message**: Clear guidance with examples of valid plugin names
- **Documentation**: Updated both `packages/docs/docs/cli/plugins.md` and `packages/cli/README.md`
- **No Breaking Changes**: Only adds validation and clarifies existing behavior

## Testing
- Manual testing confirms empty strings now show clear error instead of installing random plugins
- Help text accurately reflects v1.x default behavior
- All existing functionality preserved

- **Documentation**
  - Updated descriptions for the plugins list command to clarify that it displays v1.x plugins by default.
  - Revised CLI documentation and examples to accurately reflect the default behavior of listing v1.x plugins.

63:T6bb,**Problem**

CLI plugin loading was failing with errors like export 'State' not found in './state' and export 'ActionExample' not found in './actionExample'. This prevented @elizaos/plugin-sql and other plugins from loading, causing the CLI to fail with "Database adapter not initialized" errors. This was causing basic stuff like elizaos start/dev to fail with default char. 

**Root Cause**

The @elizaos/core package build configuration had two issues:
- Missing entry points: tsup.config.ts only included 3 entry points, but the v1 specs used relative imports like import { State } from './state'. Since state.ts wasn't an entry point, no state.js file was built, causing runtime module resolution failures.
- Type vs runtime exports: TypeScript types like State were being exported as runtime values (export { State }), but they only exist at compile-time, causing build errors when tsup tried to create JavaScript exports for non-existent runtime values.

**Solution**

Added all v1 spec files as entry points in tsup.config.ts so they get built into importable .js modules
Changed type exports to use export type { } syntax to keep types in the type-only namespace while preserving function exports as runtime exports.

<!-- This is an auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->

## Summary by CodeRabbit

- **Refactor**
  - Improved internal structure by distinguishing between type-only and value exports, enhancing module clarity without affecting the user-facing API.

- **Chores**
  - Updated build configuration to include additional entry points for better modularization. No changes to user-facing features.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->64:T1170,# Overall Project Weekly Summary (Jun 1 - 7, 2025)

## Executive Summary
This week, we made foundational improvements to the ElizaOS framework by overhauling our core messaging system for greater stability and modularity. We also significantly enhanced the user experience with a focus on mobile responsiveness and a more intuitive chat interface, making the platform more accessible and reliable for both users and developers.

### Key Strategic Initiatives & Outcomes

**Strengthening the Core Framework for Modularity and Reliability**
*Strategic Importance: This work is crucial for our long-term vision of a stable, scalable system where autonomous agents can interact reliably without interference, directly supporting our core philosophy of modularity and autonomy.*
- The core message server was completely re-architected to operate independently from agents, a key step towards a more robust and composable system in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).
- We resolved critical bugs that caused agents to get stuck in infinite communication loops, significantly improving agent stability and preventing system-wide interference in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).

**Enhancing the User Experience Across Devices**
*Strategic Importance: Making our platform intuitive and accessible on any device is key to growing our user base and ensuring a seamless experience for interacting with AI agents.*
- The user interface was upgraded to be fully responsive, providing a high-quality experience for users on mobile devices in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).
- Several improvements were made to the chat interface, including a message retry button and better data persistence, making conversations more reliable and user-friendly in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).

**Improving the Developer and Plugin Ecosystem**
*Strategic Importance: Empowering our community to build and integrate new capabilities through plugins is fundamental to our mission of creating an extensible, open-source framework.*
- We improved the plugin system by adding core specifications and making the installation process more user-friendly, lowering the barrier for developers to contribute in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).
- Our development and testing tools were upgraded, including a migration to a more reliable testing framework, to help developers build and validate their contributions more efficiently in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).

## Repository Spotlights

### elizaos/eliza
This week, the core framework saw over 65 PRs merged, focusing on architectural refactoring, UI/UX enhancements, and developer experience. These changes lay the groundwork for a more stable, modular, and user-friendly platform.

**Key Updates:**
- **Architecture & Stability:**
  - Completely refactored the message server to operate independently from agents, improving system modularity and reliability ([#4864](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/4864)).
  - Fixed critical agent cross-interference issues that were causing infinite response loops ([#4935](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/4935), [#4934](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/4934)).
  - Resolved a long-standing issue where the Twitter bot would not respond to mentions, restoring a key integration ([#4272](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/4272)).

- **User Experience:**
  - Implemented a responsive design for mobile devices with an upgrade to tailwind v4, significantly improving the user experience on smaller screens ([#4866](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/4866)).
  - Added a retry button for user messages in the chat UI, improving usability when sends fail ([#4973](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/4973)).

- **Plugin & Developer Ecosystem:**
  - Added core plugin specifications ([#4851](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/4851)) and enhanced the installation process with environment variable prompting ([#4945](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/4945)) to better support third-party developers.
